338848 Let $G$ be a network, and call a vertex $i$ a **proper source** if it has no incoming edges and is not isolated in the relevant network. An **attractor** is a dynamical attractor of the network, and a neuron is active in an attractor if it participates in that attractor's activity. Proper sources always die and are never active in an attractor of a network.

**Proper-source death conjecture.** Proper sources always die and are never active in an attractor of a network.

The source gives examples in which a proper source is absent from the global attractor and notes that proper sources do not participate in fixed-point supports. The general claim about all attractors remains open in the supplied text.
338849 Let a linkage matching field be a collection of matchings satisfying the linkage conditions, and let its maximal tope linkage covectors be the maximal covectors associated to its topes. The **combinatorial Stiefel map** associates to each such covector a transversal matroid.

**Stiefel-map determination conjecture.** A linkage matching field is determined by the collection of transversal matroids associated to its maximal tope linkage covectors by the combinatorial Stiefel map.

This conjecture proposes that the transversal matroids obtained from maximal tope linkage covectors retain enough information to recover the entire linkage matching field. The source gives no resolution or evidence establishing the claim.
338865 For positive integers $n_1,ldots,n_t$, let $P_{n_i}$ denote the path graph on $n_i$ vertices, and let $times$ denote the Cartesian product of graphs. The graph

$$
P_{n_1}times P_{n_2}times cdots times P_{n_t}
$$

is a higher-dimensional grid graph.

**Higher-dimensional grid conjecture.** The graph $P_{n_1}times P_{n_2}times cdots times P_{n_t}$ is neighborhood-prime.

The paper proves the claim for two-dimensional grids and for three-dimensional grids whose order is divisible by $4$. It proposes the conjecture as an extension to all higher-dimensional grids; no resolution is given.
338866 Let $G$ be a Hamiltonian graph of order $n$, meaning that $G$ contains a cycle through all $n$ vertices.

**Hamiltonian edge-density conjecture.** If

$$
|E(G)|>n+\frac{n}{3},
$$

then $G$ is neighborhood-prime.

The paper proves stronger sufficient conditions in some settings, including a bound derived from Hamiltonian chords, and notes that the conjecture is motivated by known results for Hamiltonian graphs of orders not congruent to $2$ modulo $4$. Its resolution is not given.
338867 For positive integers $k_1,\ldots,k_\ell$ and a positive integer $n$, let $C_m$ denote the cycle graph of length $m$, and let $\cup$ denote disjoint union.

**Cycle-union conjecture.** The union

$$
C_{4k_1}\cup C_{4k_2}\cup\cdots\cup C_{4k_\ell}\cup C_n
$$

is neighborhood-prime if $n\equiv 0\pmod{4}$ or if $n$ is odd.

The conjecture is presented as a consequence that would follow from an affirmative solution of the Deretsky–Lee–Miller prime-labeling conjecture, together with the paper's neighborhood-graph theorem. The source does not report a resolution.
338892 Let $\mathcal{C}$ be a permutation class. Write $\overline{\operatorname{gr}}(\mathcal{C})$ for its upper growth rate and $\overline{\operatorname{gr}}^{rc}(\mathcal{C})$ for the upper $rc$-growth rate, obtained from the even-size centrosymmetric permutations in $\mathcal{C}$. **Centrosymmetric growth-rate conjecture.**

$$
\overline{\operatorname{gr}}^{rc}(\mathcal{C})\leq\overline{\operatorname{gr}}(\mathcal{C}).
$$

The conjecture asserts that the growth of even-size centrosymmetric elements cannot exceed the overall growth of the class. The paper proves the reverse inequality in several settings, including sum-closed $rc$-invariant classes and certain geometric grid classes, but the general inequality remains open.
338893 Let $\mathcal{C}$ be a sum-closed and $rc$-invariant permutation class. Let $\operatorname{gr}(\mathcal{C})$ denote its growth rate and $\operatorname{gr}^{rc}(\mathcal{C})$ the growth rate of its even-size centrosymmetric elements, when the latter exists. **Sum-closed centrosymmetric growth-rate conjecture.** The $rc$-growth rate exists and

$$
\operatorname{gr}^{rc}(\mathcal{C})=\operatorname{gr}(\mathcal{C}).
$$

The paper proves the inequality $\underline{\operatorname{gr}}^{rc}(\mathcal{C})\geq\operatorname{gr}(\mathcal{C})$ for such classes; this conjecture would follow from the general centrosymmetric growth-rate inequality and remains open.
339010 Let $M$ be a matroid on $[d]:=\{1,\ldots,d\}$, with bases $\mathcal{B}$, and let

$$
P(M):=\operatorname{conv}\left(\sum_{i\in B}{\boldsymbol{e}}_i:B\in\mathcal{B}\right)\subset\mathbb{R}^d
$$

be its matroid polytope. A lattice polytope is **Ehrhart positive** if all coefficients of its Ehrhart polynomial are positive, and **Ehrhart unimodal** if its Ehrhart $h^\ast$-polynomial has a unimodal coefficient sequence.

**Deloera–Haws–Köppe conjecture.** For any matroid $M$, the matroid polytope $P(M)$ is both Ehrhart positive and Ehrhart unimodal.

Both aspects of this conjecture were described as elusive, with only partial results known; the conjecture concerns a broad family beyond the zonotopes already known to have both properties.
339036 Let $a,b$ be relatively prime positive integers, with one of $a,b$ even. For an Ulam sequence $\mathcal{U}(a,b)$, **Finch's even-term conjecture.** If $a$ is even, then $\mathcal{U}(a,b)$ has $2+a/2$ even terms, precisely $\{a,a+2b,\ldots,a+ab,(2a+4)b\}$; if $b$ is even, then it has $2+b/2$ even terms, precisely $\{b,b+2a,\ldots,b+ba,(2b+4)a\}$. Earlier results in the paper establish lower bounds and the initial families of even terms; the source presents Finch's assertion as an open conjecture.
339051 Let $k$ be an integer, and let $\cal F$ be a **sunflower-free** $k$-uniform set system, meaning that it contains no sunflower with the relevant number of petals.

**Bounded subcover conjecture.** There exist $F_1,\ldots,F_{2k}\in\cal F$ such that

$$
\bigcup_{F\in\cal F}F=\bigcup_{j=1}^{2k}F_j.
$$

The source presents this as a weaker version of the preceding union-size conjecture. No resolution is given in the source.
339069 Let $X$ be a class of permutations, and let ${\cal G}_X$ be the corresponding class of permutation graphs. A permutation class is **geometrically griddable** when it can be represented by a geometric grid class.

**Geometric griddability and finite-letter-graph conjecture.** $X$ is geometrically griddable if and only if ${\cal G}_X$ is a class of $k$-letter graphs for some finite value of $k$.

This conjecture proposes that geometric grid classes of permutations and finite-letter graphs describe the same underlying structure through permutation graphs. The paper establishes constructive polynomial-time recognition for $3$-letter graphs, but the conjectured equivalence is not stated as resolved here.
339071 Let $n=2k$ and let $\mathcal{F}\subset2^{[n]}$ be intersecting. Define

$$
\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{F})=|\mathcal{F}|-\max_{x\in[n]}|\{F\in\mathcal{F}:x\in F\}|.
$$

**Even-order diversity conjecture.** If $k$ is not a power of $2$, then

$$
\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{F})\le\frac12\binom{2k-1}{k-1}+\binom{2k-1}{k+1}+\cdots+\binom{2k-1}{2k-1};
$$

and if $k$ is a power of $2$, then

$$
\operatorname{div}(\mathcal{F})\le\frac12\left(\binom{2k-1}{k-1}-1\right)+\binom{2k-1}{k+1}+\cdots+\binom{2k-1}{2k-1}.
$$

This conjecture addresses the non-uniform diversity problem in the even case, where the middle-level $k$-sets require a regular intersecting subfamily and the possible extremal value depends on whether $k$ is a power of $2$.
339092 Let $G$ be a bipartite graph in which every vertex in one part has degree $a$ and every vertex in the other part has degree $b$; such a graph is called **$(a,b)$-biregular**. A cyclic interval $k$-coloring is a proper edge coloring with colors arranged cyclically so that the colors incident with each vertex form a cyclic interval.

**Casselgren–Toft's conjecture.** Every $(a,b)$-biregular graph admits a cyclic interval $\max\{a,b\}$-coloring.

Regular bipartite graphs have cyclic interval colorings, and this conjecture asks whether the corresponding optimal number of colors always exists for biregular graphs. The source cites further work for information about its status, but the supplied text does not establish a resolution.
339158 Let $\mathcal{P}$ be a $d$-complete poset with bottom tree $\mathcal{B}$. Let $T$ be a skew increasing $\mathcal{P}$-tableau, and let $R$ and $S$ be rectifications of $T$. **Bottom-tree rectification conjecture.** The restrictions of the rectifications to the bottom tree agree:

$$
R|_{\mathcal{B}}=S|_{\mathcal{B}}.
$$

This generalizes the proved unique-rectification result for chained double-tailed diamond posets and provides additional experimental evidence toward the broader unique-rectification-target conjecture. It asserts invariance on the bottom tree even when the full rectification need not yet be known to be unique.
339185 Let $M_1$ be a matroid with a **circuit-hyperplane** $H$, and let $M_2$ be obtained from $M_1$ by relaxing $H$. If both $M_1$ and $M_2$ are representable over a finite field $\mathbb F$, then the branch-width of $M_1$ should be bounded by a constant depending only upon $|\mathbb F|$. This conjecture is the second conjecture used in the reported proof of Rota's Conjecture, and the paper's main result shows that it implies the corresponding fragile-matroid branch-width conjecture. The source reports that its proof was also reported by Geelen, Gerards, and Whittle.
339209 Let $G=P_n$ be the path graph on $n$ cells, and let $\mathcal D_{G,\ell}$ denote the collection of transformations whose invariant subspaces are being classified by the algorithm in the preceding example. **Path-graph completeness conjecture.** The algorithm in the previous example gives all of the $\mathcal D_{G,\ell}$-invariant subspaces for the path $G=P_n$. This conjecture asserts completeness of the proposed classification algorithm for invariant subspaces of path networks; the source provides no resolution.
339214 Let $\Gamma$ be a homogeneous finite-dimensional permutation structure, and call it **fully generic** when it is the generic structure obtained from the relevant amalgamation class.

**3-type realization conjecture.** If $\Gamma$ realizes all 3-types, then $\Gamma$ is fully generic.

This is presented as one part of the Primitivity Conjecture and is retained for consideration via direct amalgamation arguments; the source gives no resolution.
339215 Let $\Gamma$ be a primitive homogeneous finite-dimensional permutation structure. A **realized 2-type** is a 2-type realized in $\Gamma$, and a 3-type involves such a 2-type when its restriction to a pair is realized in $\Gamma$.

**3-type extension conjecture.** All 3-types involving realized 2-types are realized in $\Gamma$.

This is presented as the second part of the Primitivity Conjecture; the source gives no resolution.
339216 Let $E$ be a $\emptyset$-definable equivalence relation in a finite-dimensional permutation structure, and let $C_1$ and $C_2$ be $E$-classes. Naming $C_1$ means expanding the structure by a predicate for $C_1$.

**Homogeneity-after-naming conjecture.** The class $C_2$ remains homogeneous after naming $C_1$.

The statement is identified as a possible route to ruling out counterexamples to the conjectural classification and is known in the case of three linear orders; the general claim remains open.
339267 Let $G$ be a bridgeless cubic graph, let $e$ and $f$ be adjacent edges, and let $i,j$ be integers satisfying $0\leq i,j\leq 2$ and $1\leq i+j\leq 3$. An **FR-triple** is a list $C=(F_1,F_2,F_3)$ of perfect matchings with $F_1\cap F_2\cap F_3=\emptyset$, and let $\nu_C(e)$ denote the number of members of $C$ containing $e$.

**Adjacent-edge multiplicity conjecture.** The graph $G$ contains an FR-triple $C$ such that

$$
\nu_C(e)=i\qquad\text{and}\qquad\nu_C(f)=j.
$$

This is the second conjecture considered in the paper, prescribing multiplicities on two adjacent edges. The source does not give evidence that it has been resolved.
